# Depthcrawl patch notes

## RS-544 - Armour on the arms, across every build
- **Mail sleeves:** a mail hauberk now has mail sleeves to the elbow. Before, the mail stopped at the shoulders and the arms were bare cloth.
- **Skirts fit heavy bodies:** the lower skirt of a jerkin or hauberk is cut wider for a heavy build. A heavy body's hips and thighs used to come through it, leaving a flap at the front.
- **Arms hang clear:** on a heavy body, wide hips or big muscles, the arms hang a little further out from the sides. They used to sink into the hips and belly.

## RS-543 - Every hairstyle, done properly
- **Braids** are plaited from three strands crossing over each other, with a gold bead and a tassel at the end. They were smooth tubes.
- **Dreadlocks** are twisted ropes growing from all over the back and sides, falling to the shoulders. They were stiff cylinders.
- **Curls** are coiled loops standing off the head all over. They were a cluster of balls.
- **The mohawk** is a crest of hair standing up along the middle of a shaved head. It was a row of cones.
- **The topknot** is hair combed up from all round to a bun wound at the crown, with its tie. It was a ball on a crop.
- **Under helmets:** braids and dreadlocks fall from under a helmet, as long hair does.
- **Ponytail sides** rise toward the tie, each lock its own way, instead of running round the head as a band.
- **Soft hairlines:** swept hair, ponytails and topknots end in loose short strands at the nape instead of a neat line.

## RS-542 - The creator shows the body
- **Underclothes in the creator:** while you make your character, they stand in plain linen underclothes. The cloak, collar, tunic, hat and boots are gone, including each race's own costume, so you can see the build sliders and the hair at work. Your shirt and trouser choices still apply once you're in the game.
- **Fitted to the body:** the briefs, and a band round a woman's breasts, are cut from the body's own surface. They fit any build and follow a wood elf's stoop.
- **Bare feet with toes:** plain bare feet are slim and have toes. They used to look like slippers with white tufts on top. Halflings keep their big hairy feet.

## RS-541 - Plate that fits the limbs in it
- **No more doll joints:** on a slight build, the knee and elbow plates were balls wider than the leg and arm, like a jointed doll. Knee plates now cup the front of the knee, with small side wings, and elbow plates cup the back of the elbow.
- **Greaves have a calf:** shin plates follow the shape of the leg, swelling at the calf and narrowing to the ankle. They used to be straight pipes.
- **Sized to the build:** thigh plates, forearm plates and greaves sit just off the limb inside them. A slender fighter's forearm plates are no longer wide tubes, and a heavy fighter's armour grows with their build.

## RS-540 - Hair that moves
- **Long hair swings:** it trails behind as you set off, swings forward when you stop and swings out as you turn. It settles on a spring instead of being frozen in place. Ponytails and beards move too; beards move less.
- **Wind:** on the open land, hair streams with the same wind that blows the cloaks, harder in a storm, and the ends flutter.
- **It hangs down:** when a head tips forward or back, the hair still falls toward the ground.
- **Everyone has their own:** each person's hair moves with them, and someone standing still isn't swayed by you walking past. The crown stays put; only the hanging part moves.

## RS-539 - Hair, finished
- **No more flat fringe:** the scalp under the hair used to sit above the hair cards, so it showed as a flat slab on the brow and a bare dome at the crown. It now follows the hairline and lies beneath the cards.
- **Long hair parts:** it's combed down and aside from a parting to the temples, and it covers the ears instead of letting them poke through.
- **No seam at the back:** long hair leaves the head at its widest point and falls clear. It used to hug the head down to the nape and then kink outward, leaving a line across the back of the head.
- **Swept hair is whole:** it runs back from the brow over the top in lanes and covers the back of the head. Half of it was missing.
- **Ponytails are combed back** to the tie from all round the hairline, with a fuller tail. The front used to be a fringe and the tie a black knot.
- **Hair under helmets:** long hair and ponytails now fall from under hats and helmets, though not from under a great helm or a hood. They used to vanish.
- **Hair shines in the world:** characters out in the world had lost the hair highlight. They have it back.
- **Softer roots:** the dense part near the roots now ends unevenly, not in a jagged line across the head.

## RS-538 - Armour that fits the body in it

- **Belts round the body**: a belt was the size of the build everyone used to have, so on a heavy body it was buried and on a slight one it floated. It's now measured round the body wearing it.
- **Robes that fit**: a robe's waist, sash and skirt were the same for every build, and a heavy body's belly hung out over the skirt like a shelf. The robe now meets the body at its own waist and flares from there.
- **Tabards on the body**: a tabard was a flat card that a belly or a broad chest pushed through. It's now laid over the chest and belly as they are, and hangs straight down from where it leaves them.

## RS-537 - Hair, the modern way

- **Hair cards**: hair was a bundle of solid tubes, and it looked it. It's now drawn the way today's games draw hair - cards: thin ribbons laid in combed rows from the crown down over the head, each carrying a painted clump of a hundred fine strands with gaps between them, cut out so you see through them, tapering to soft tips. Three layers - the body of it, one between, and a few loose strands lifting off the top - so no scalp shows and it doesn't sit like a helmet.
- **Soft edges**: every card is drawn twice - a solid core, and a see-through pass over it - so the ends and edges are fine and soft, not jagged.
- **Hair shine**: the highlight on hair runs across the strands (the Kajiya-Kay shading games use), a bright band and a dimmer one tinted by the hair's colour, sliding over the head as the light and your view move.
- **Every style**: a crop combed down to the hairline; long hair falling to rest on the shoulders and on down the back, the front combed aside off the face; swept hair running back from the brow; a ponytail gathered at the back. Beards and moustaches are cards too.

## RS-536 - Beasts fall on their side

- **No more rump in the air**: a beast going down rolls over sideways, but the roll followed the blow - struck along its length, from the front or behind, a bear, boar or owlbear was pitched forward onto its chest with its back end hoisted up. It now always goes over across its spine, onto its side, whichever way the blow came, its hips and chest rolling together; and the hips are kept square to the spine, so they can't tip the rump up on their own.
- **Legs out of the body**: the body a beast's legs must stay outside was sized from how far apart its hips are - on a bulky body, well inside the belly, so the legs folded up into it (and the folded legs propped the body up). It's now measured from the creature's own skin. A dragon's legs, which had no such rule at all, now have it too.
- **No flung corpses**: once in a long while a corpse could be thrown clean out of the room; a body that would leave the room in one step now stays where it is.

## RS-535 - Corpses that lie still, torches on the walls

- **Corpses settle**: a heavy body that died the right way up - a giant vulture, slumped belly down - never quite counted as still, and the slow turn it was left with dragged it creeping round in a circle across the floor for as long as it lay there. The floor now takes that turn out of it, and it comes to rest.
- **Torches on straight walls**: dressing a level after its torches were hung could open floor beside one, leaving a torch on a corner or a pillar. Any torch that ends up off a straight wall now comes down.
- **Behind the scenes**: nineteen of the game's own checks had fallen out of date with the changes of the last many releases (skinned figures, the waist bend, the shared resolution scaler, bows in the left hand, one-piece robes and more). They're brought up to date, so a real breakage shows up again - and two of them found the bugs above.

## RS-534 - Open hands and made boots

- **An open hand**: your off hand was always a clenched fist round nothing. When it's empty it now rests open - the palm, the fingers a little curled, the thumb alongside. It still closes on a shield, a tome, an off-hand blade, a bow, or the grip of a two-handed weapon or quarterstaff.
- **Boots**: shod feet were a simple rounded shape; now they're a cobbler's boot - a rounded toe box, a stitched sole standing out from the upper all round, a heel block, a folded cuff at the top and a seam down the front. Bare feet keep their own shape.

## RS-533 - Hair that looks like hair

- **A finish of its own**: hair, brows and beards used the same speckled finish as an animal's fur, so a head of hair read like a knitted cap. Now they have a hair finish - fine streaks that run down each lock and back from the crown, with a soft sheen where the light catches it.
- **Finer locks**: half as many again, slimmer, and each drawn to a fine point instead of ending blunt; beards the same.
- Wolves, bears and other beasts keep their fur.

## RS-532 - Eyes of your own

- **Eye sliders**: two more in the FACE section - the size of the eyes and how far apart they're set. The socket, the lids and the eyeball move together, so the eyes always sit in the face; at the middle of each slider they're where they were.

## RS-531 - Muscle that shows

- **The muscle slider does its job**: it barely changed anything - a few percent on the arms. Now, from soft to hard, it fills out the chest, raises and thickens the trapezius, rounds the deltoids, broadens the lats and swells the upper arms, forearms, thighs and calves: about half as thick again in the arm from one end to the other. Hard muscle stands out under a tunic too - sleeves and hose are cut to the arms and legs inside them.
- The middle of the slider is the build you had, so nobody's figure changes unless you move it. Figures are re-sculpted once for the new shapes.

## RS-530 - No cards stuck on the face

- **The chin**: a human man's square jaw was a flat box whose corners stuck out as pale wedges either side of the chin; it's now sculpted into the jaw itself - wider at the base, a stronger chin.
- **Scars and war paint**: they were flat strips that floated off the cheek; now each one is laid onto the face and follows its curve, so a scar is a line in the skin and paint is painted on.
- **Moustaches**: the moustache on the braided, forked and dwarf beards was two little boxes; it's now a sweep of strands from under the nose out over the lip and down past the corners of the mouth.

## RS-529 - Shape your face

- **Face sliders**: a FACE section under the build in the character creator - jaw, chin, cheeks, brow, nose length and width, mouth, lips and age. Each face is its own sculpt, in the strong-nosed, deep-eyed style of the old cartoons; at the middle of every slider (and age a young adult's) the face is the one you had.
- **Age**: from a young adult on, lines come - across the forehead, the folds from the nose to the corners of the mouth, a little fullness under the eyes - deeper the older you set it.
- **The preview closes in**: shaping a face zooms the turning figure in on it; **look closer** toggles the zoom, and there's a **random face** and a **reset**.
- **At the barber's too**, for nothing, like the build.

## RS-528 - Skin that isn't plastic

- **Light into skin**: skin now takes light the way real skin does - a little way in and back out warm. Where you're lit, the edges turning away glow faintly warm, and the shadowed side is lifted with a ruddy light instead of falling to grey. It's a touch less glossy too. Every face, hand and bare arm in the game, players, townsfolk and monsters alike.
- **A flush of warmth**: the cheeks, the tip of the nose, the ears and the lips are rosier than the rest of the face, as blood shows through. On humans, elves, dwarves, halflings and the other folk, orcs, goblins and hags - not on golems, skeletons and the like.
- The faces are re-sculpted once for the new colours (a moment on first sight, cached after).

## RS-527 - The whole of you in the preview

- **Head to foot**: the turning figure in the character creator, the barber's chair and the dye works is now framed to the figure as it stands - hat, horns, ears and boots included - so a tall wood elf no longer loses her feet and hat off the edges, and a gnome isn't lost in empty space. Zooming in still closes on the face.

## RS-526 - A tidier creation screen

- **Races in a picker**: instead of ten rows to read through, the race is one card - its name, what it is, its gift and the skills it's good at - with arrows either side to step through them, and a dot for each race you can tap to jump straight there. The arrow keys and the number keys pick a race too.
- **No stats row**: the row of starting skill levels at the bottom (all 1s and a 10) is gone - it was the same for everyone.

## RS-525 - Build your body

- **Body sliders**: the character creator has a BUILD section under your appearance - thirteen sliders for frame, weight, muscle, shoulders, chest, waist, hips, neck, head, legs, arms, height and posture. The number follows your finger as you drag; the figure is sculpted afresh the moment you let go, a few milliseconds a frame, so the screen never freezes and the figure you had stands until the new one is ready ("sculpting..." under it meanwhile).
- **Presets**: Average, Ranger, Scholar, Brute, Dancer, Veteran, Waif and Giant, one tap each; a **random build**; and **reset**. The lit preset tells you which one you're on - none lit means it's your own.
- **At the barber's too**: an existing character can set their build in the barber's chair, for nothing. Hair and clothes still cost what they did.
- The old Lean / Average / Stocky row is gone; the frame slider has it. An older character's build carries over.

## RS-524 - Bodies built to a build

- **Real anatomy**: every humanoid is sculpted again with the figure of Bakshi's rotoscoped film in mind - collarbones with the hollows above them, deltoids that cap the arm, lats broad under the arms tapering to a waist, a neck that is a neck, a firmer chest, a spine with its groove, and the belly, flanks and seat that fill out with weight. The default build is the shape everyone had, so your character, the townsfolk and the monsters keep their look - just better made.
- **A build of your own**: a body is now built from twelve smooth settings - frame, weight, muscle, shoulders, chest, waist, hips, neck, head, legs, arms, height - and posture. A lean, hard-muscled narrow-waisted fighter, a heavy one with a belly and love handles, a slight woman with a small bust, a full one with wide hips: each is its own sculpt, and the garments (tunics, jerkins, hauberks, breastplates, robes) are sculpted over the same masses, so they fit every build with nothing poking through. The sliders to set them come next; an old character's Lean, Average or Stocky build carries over as the frame.
- **The sculpt cache turns over**: shapes changed, so sculpted parts are made afresh the first time you see them (a moment longer on first sight, cached after as before).

## RS-523 - A wizard's shield, held like a shield

- **Staff and shield**: carrying a shield with a staff, your off hand reached across to grip the staff as if it were two-handed, leaving the shield held at your chest like a book. The staff is now held in one hand and the shield is raised on your other arm, like any other shield. A staff on its own is still held in both hands.

## RS-522 - Robes like Gandalf's

- **Heavy wool**: robes now move like Gandalf's grey in Bakshi's *The Lord of the Rings* - they follow your walk lazily and trail further behind, the hem does the swinging while the hips stay calm, and when you stop they make one slow swing and hang still within about a second. No more bounce.
- **Finer cloth each tier**: plain, Splitbark and Mystic robes are heavy wool; Infinity is a little lighter; Ahrim's is dark cloth that billows wider and swings on longer before it settles.
- **Wind**: out on the land and in town streets the hem stirs in the breeze even while you stand still - a little on a clear day, more in rain, and a storm's gusts push it well out to one side. Indoors and underground the air is still.

## RS-521 - Smoother walks when the map changes

- **No stall when the land changes far off**: when a tile changed somewhere on the map - a cleared site reclaimed by the wild, a new one rising, a bridge mended - the game worked out the land's regions and landmarks, the distant ground and the near land's shading each in one go, mid-walk. They're now each worked out a little a frame, with the old ones shown in the meantime, and the towns and sites in the land you're walking into are built one per step instead of all together.
- **The numbers**: at a phone's pace, the worst frame after such a change fell from about 360ms to under 100ms. Everything comes out exactly as before - the same regions, names, landmarks and light on the land.

## RS-520 - No hitch when a new creature comes into sight

- **New creatures built in slices**: the first time a kind of creature came into view out on the land, building its model cost a whole frame per sculpted part - 80 to 110ms at a phone's pace, many times over for a big one - and re-ran the whole build every frame until it was done (40ms a frame for seconds, over 12s in all for a hill giant). Now each sculpted part is itself made a few milliseconds a frame, and the build runs once to see what it needs and once for real. At a phone's pace the worst frame for a new kind fell from about 100ms to 20-45ms, and a new kind turns up in under a second instead of one to twelve.
- **Faster skinning**: joining a creature's parts into one bending mesh now works straight on the vertex data, a good deal quicker for every creature built.
- Shapes are exactly as before - nothing to re-download or re-sculpt.

## RS-519 - Crossbow scouts and the Archer's quiver

- **Duergar scouts carry crossbows**: they held bows before; now they hold a crossbow, posed and drawn as one in the world and in the bestiary, and their shots fly as bolts, loosed when the string lets go rather than before.
- **A quiver for older Archers**: Archers made before the kit came with a leather quiver now get one, once - worn if your cape slot is free, otherwise in your bag. Anyone who already has a quiver, and every other kit, is left alone.

## RS-518 - Tomes and the ranged button

- **Tomes drawn like the rest**: a tome's icon is now a closed book in its dye with cream page edges and leather grain, finer each tier - corner caps from Splitbark, glowing runes round the border from Mystic, gold corners and a clasp with your chosen device (or a star) inlaid from Infinity, and a glowing green gem on Ahrim's.
- **The ranged button shows your weapon**: the hotbar's ranged button used one stock bow for everything; it now shows the very weapon you carry - your crossbow in its metal, your magic longbow, your darts or chinchompas.

## RS-517 - Weapons and jewellery, redrawn

- **Every weapon its own icon, in its own metal**: daggers, swords, longswords, 2h swords, scimitars, maces, warhammers, battleaxes, spears and halberds each have their own shape now - the halberd no longer looks like a spear - drawn in their metal and finer each tier: polished from steel, a gilded guard from mithril, a gold-wired grip from adamant, a gem in the pommel on rune and dragon, and dragon's dark serrated spine and spiked guard.
- **Bows by their wood**: plain to magic, with gold nocks and a wired grip on yew and glowing runes on magic; the Dark Bow, Crystal Bow, Twisted Bow and Seercull each have their own look. **Crossbows** are drawn as crossbows - stock, prod in its metal, string and bolt.
- **Staves by grade**: a knobbed staff with its orb, a banded battlestaff, a mystic staff's twisted shaft and claw head, and a master or ancient staff gilded with runes circling a glowing orb - the orb in its element's colour.
- **Thrown weapons**: a fan of darts, crossed throwing knives, chinchompas as chinchompas.
- **Jewellery**: every ring shows its own gem - sapphire, emerald, ruby, diamond, dragonstone - with Fremennik rings on carved bands, and every amulet its own pendant: glory's rays, fury's gold frame and onyx, the occult necklace's glowing eye, and the holy and unholy symbols.

## RS-516 - Robes that move like cloth

- **Cloth physics on robes**: a robe's skirt is now carried with momentum after the shape your legs give it. It trails behind you as you walk (about 7 cm at the hem), flares out when you turn, and swings back and settles when you stop. Neighbouring folds share the motion, so it moves in waves rather than as a stiff bell; the waist stays put and the hem swings most.
- **Your legs stay inside it**: the cloth may billow out but hardly presses in, so even trailing behind a long stride the legs never push through it (checked every few frames of a walk in the new test).
- **For you and those near you**: townsfolk further off keep the plain pose, and sitting still folds the robe over the seat as before.
- **Cheap**: about a quarter of a millisecond a frame per robe; in the tiefling city on the audit save, with six robed figures near you, the frame time didn't change (11.5 ms).

## RS-515 - A cleaner worn-gear panel

- **Just the description**: tapping a piece you're wearing now shows only its description - no take off or close buttons (drag it off the figure to take it off, as before), and no "replaces" comparison, which only made sense for something in your bag. Tapping a bag item still shows what it would replace.

## RS-514 - Worn gear described

- **Tap a worn piece to read it**: on the equipment page, tapping a piece on your figure now describes it at the bottom - its name, stats and the rest - just as tapping something in your bag does, with a **take off** button to move it into the bag. Tapping the bag again shows that item instead; whichever you tapped last is the one described.

## RS-513 - The paper doll, redrawn

- **Every piece of gear has its own icon**: helms, bodies, legs, gloves, boots, capes and shields used one icon per slot whatever they were - a wizard's robe got the same picture as a rune platebody. Each is now drawn for what it is, plate, dragonhide or cloth, and finer each tier the way the figure wears it:
  - **Plate**: a great helm whose bands, plumes, wings and horns follow the tiers; a platebody with pauldrons and a fauld, rivets, trim, a tabard bearing its device and dragon spikes; articulated gauntlets, sabatons, platelegs with knee cops.
  - **Dragonhide**: a hooded coif stitched over the crown, gaining studs, a feather, scales and spines; a laced jerkin with studs, trim and a fur collar; laced chaps, bracers and tall boots.
  - **Cloth**: a wizard's hat with its band, gem, stars, moon and - on Ahrim's - a crooked tip; the full robe with bell sleeves, a sash, its trim and hem runes and Ahrim's mantle; soft gloves and curled shoes.
  - **Capes** with their hem band, gold edging, embroidery, fur collar and your emblem; the fire cape in lava; quivers as quivers. **Shields**, kite and tower, with their rim, your device, the boss and spikes.
- **In your colours**: every icon shows the piece's dye and trim dye, and capes and shields show the emblem you chose.

## RS-512 - A trousers icon

- **Trousers look like trousers**: cloth trousers have their own icon - a gathered waistband with its drawstring, two legs with soft folds and a gap between - in the item's own colour or dye. Like the trousers themselves it grows finer each tier: ankle cuffs from Splitbark, seam runes from Mystic, gold from Infinity, a double cuff and green runes on Ahrim's, and a dyed trim shows on it too. They borrowed the plate legs' icon before.

## RS-511 - Trousers finer each tier

- **The tier trims are back, on the trousers**: as the robe bottoms did, the trousers now grow finer with each tier - Splitbark gains a trim cuff at each ankle, Mystic adds glowing runes stitched down the outer seams, Infinity turns cuffs and runes gold, and Ahrim's adds a second cuff and green-glowing runes. Wizard trousers stay plain.
- **Dyeable**: from Splitbark up, the trousers get a trim row at the dye works, and the cuffs and seam runes take the dye.
- Under a full robe only the hem shows; worn with leather or plate on top, the trousers show their finery in full.

## RS-510 - A trim for every robe

- **Every robe takes a trim dye**: the dye works now offers a trim row for any robe. Before, only robes from Splitbark up had a trim, so a Wizard's or a Monk's robe had no trim option at all. Dyeing a plain robe's trim gives it a trim band at the hem and down the front in that colour; washing it out takes the band away again.

## RS-509 - The full robe

- **Robes reach the ankles again**: the robe is one piece from the collar to the hem - its skirt, the trim band and the glowing hem runes all its own - whatever you wear on your legs, plate and leather included. It used to be a waist-length robe top over a separate robe bottom's skirt, so a robe top over plate legs had no skirt at all.
- **Robe bottoms are now trousers**: cloth trousers in the item's own colour, showing at the ankles under a robe. Same stats, new name and look - "Mystic Robe Bottom" is now "Mystic Trousers", and its icon is a pair of trousers.
- **Your old pieces renamed**: everything you carry, wear or keep in the bank is renamed on load - "Mystic Robe Top" becomes "Mystic Robe", a "Monk's Robe Bottom" "Monk's Trousers", a Holy Vestment top simply "Holy Vestment". Stats untouched.
- Next: real cloth physics on the robe's skirt, so it swings and trails as you walk and turn.

## RS-508 - A lighter HUD

- **Spell counts worked out once**: the action bar shows how many casts your runes allow for each spell, and it worked that out every frame for every spell - a search through up to thirty rune plans each. It's now kept until your bag, your staff or the turn changes, and still updates the moment you spend or pick up runes (tested).
- **No forced layout every frame**: the quest tracker and the minimap's turn buttons wrote their position and then read the page's layout straight back, every frame - which forces the browser to lay the page out again mid-frame. They now settle a few times a second, and at once when the window or the view changes. Those layout reads fell by half on the walk.

## RS-507 - Tails and tentacles, cheaper

- **Bending tubes cost a fraction**: every tail, tentacle, serpent body and tongue is a tube bent along a moving spine each frame, and each bend recomputed every vertex's lighting normal over all the tube's triangles, then re-measured its bounds. The normals now come straight from the tube's cross-section - the same direction three.js worked out, checked in the new test - and the bounds are measured every twentieth bend. Snakes and eels look exactly as before (compared side by side).
- **Measured on the uploaded save at a phone's pace**: walking the open land drew 22% more frames (246 against 202 over the same walk), and the slowest 5% of frames fell from 130 ms to 101 ms.

## RS-506 - World changes without the freeze

- **A change anywhere on the map no longer freezes the land**: when a tile changes - a cleared site reclaimed by the wild, a new one rising, a bridge mended - the open land used to rebuild all at once in a single frame, then warm every shader again. At a phone's pace that was a 1.5-second freeze, wherever you were, even if the change was 60 tiles away. It now rebuilds in the background like a walk's rebuild, and the change shows a moment later. Worst frame at a phone's pace: **1,472 ms to about 350 ms** (what's left is redrawing the map's chart, once).
- With RS-505, these were the two big sources of stutter on long walks. Measured on the uploaded save with a warm cache at a phone's pace, the slowest 5% of walking frames are now around 140 ms, from about 400 before RS-505.

## RS-505 - Long walks on the open land, smooth

- **No freeze at the end of each land rebuild**: the land is built in a 96-tile window round you, rebuilt in the background as you walk. The rebuild only started 4 tiles before the edge of the built land would come into view, and if you got there first, it finished everything in one go - a freeze of up to a second. A phone or a busy laptop always got there first, so every rebuild on a long walk ended in a stutter. It now starts 13 tiles earlier and speeds up gradually as you near the edge, finishing at once only if the edge is truly on screen. At a phone's pace: the worst rebuild frame fell from **758 ms to about 120 ms**.
- **No loading warm-up mid-walk**: the end of each background rebuild also set off a whole-scene shader warm-up meant for loading screens, which held frames for seconds on a slow device. A rebuild done over a walk already prepares its own shaders, so that warm-up now only follows a real load. On the uploaded save at a phone's pace, the slowest 5% of walking frames fell from **397 ms to 97 ms**.
- **Cheaper warm-ups everywhere**: each warm-up draw now shows only the one thing it is preparing, not the whole scene again.
- One first-time cost remains: an animal or monster seen for the very first time is sculpted then (up to a quarter of a second on a phone). The sculpt cache (RS-500) keeps it after that.

## RS-504 - Lighter town frames

- **The HUD stopped re-checking your skills every frame**: reading your bag or a skill first made sure every skill field existed - about thirty checks - and the HUD does hundreds of such reads a frame. The fields are now filled in once per character, which was about 6% of every town frame on the audit save. New and loaded characters still get every default (tested).
- **Villagers' cloaks settle with four solver passes** instead of six; yours keeps all six. Their cloth still lies on the back and drapes the same to the eye, for a little less work per cloaked villager in sight.

## RS-503 - No more hitches while walking

- **Mid-play shader warm-ups go quiet**: when a slice of land swaps in while you walk, its new shaders used to warm the way a loading screen does - holding frames and even flashing the loading card mid-stride. On a slower machine that read as massive lag spikes everywhere. A warm-up that happens mid-play now sips about 10 milliseconds a frame, never holds a frame and never shows the card; whatever it hasn't warmed yet simply appears a few frames later, far off at the window's edge. Real loads keep the card as before.
- **The land freeze sweep only sweeps changes**: the once-a-second pass that freezes placed land out of the matrix walk recomposed two thousand matrices every time; it now skips groups that haven't changed.
- **The sculpt cache writes in sips**: saving freshly sculpted models to the browser store copies their data, and several megabytes in one beat showed as a hitch; it now writes about a megabyte per beat.
- Walking frame times on the audit machine, this save: dungeon median 8.3 ms (no spikes), overworld 12.6 ms, town 29 ms - all at or better than before the audit began.

## RS-502 - The dungeon freeze, actually gone

- **A correction first**: RS-499 claimed dungeon entry no longer froze. It still did - the dungeon had two older warm-up paths of its own that compiled every shader in one blocking call before the new sliced compile ever ran, and the measurement mistook that block for build time. Reported from a real save (thank you): entering a dungeon froze for 12-13 seconds on the audit machine, and far longer on a slower one.
- **Dungeon entry now**: one task of about 3.5s (building the floor itself) and the shader work spread in slices behind the loading card, none longer than the single costliest shader (~1.7s). The dead 13-second freeze is gone. First entry on the uploaded save: a 12.8s freeze before, a 3.7s worst task after.
- **Going deeper is nearly free**: every floor used to warm all its shaders again - 13s per floor - for two reasons now fixed: the warm-up forgot what it had already drawn, and three.js bakes the number of lights into every shader, so a floor with one more portal or brazier than the last recompiled all of them. Materials are warmed once per session, and each view pads its point lights to the most it has seen with dark stand-ins, so the count holds steady. Floors below the first: 13.7s before, 0.6-1.8s after.
- **Steadier shaders across sessions too**: with light counts padded, the same shaders are generated run after run, so the browser's own compiled-shader cache can serve them on your next session.

## RS-501 - The land out of the matrix walk

- **The placed land is frozen**: every tree stump, ore vein, fishing spot and landmark had its transform matrix recomposed by the renderer every single frame, though none of them ever move. The gathering nodes and landmarks (about 2,200 objects on a busy map) are now frozen once placed and skipped by the per-frame walk entirely; their matrices stay exact (checked against a fresh compose in the new test). The wildlife, boats, light beams and fish fins keep moving as before, and a once-a-second sweep freezes whatever a chunk rebuild just added. The renderer's matrix walk on the open land fell from about 1.1 ms to 0.5 ms a frame - headroom that matters most on phones and weaker PCs.
- **Sprite sheets without the stall**: the painted creature and item sprites are drawn on a scratch canvas and then read back pixel by pixel; the canvas now says so up front, so the browser keeps it off the GPU and the readback doesn't stall. About half a second less of the first load.

## RS-500 - Sculpted models remembered between sessions

- **Models are sculpted once, ever**: every sculpted shape - bodies, heads, gear, the graveyard skeleton's bones - is carved from a distance field and meshed, which is most of what made loading slow. The finished mesh is now kept in the browser (IndexedDB) and later sessions load it back instead of carving it again, byte for byte the same (checked in the new test).
- **Measured on the audit save**: loading into a tiefling city with a warm cache, the longest freeze fell from 4.8s to 2.3s and the load's total stalled time from 16s to 9.4s - on top of RS-499's wins. The skeleton's 15 seconds of background sculpting after every load is gone too.
- **Self-managing**: the cache keeps at most 64 MB, dropping the least recently used sculpts, and a version stamp throws old shapes away whenever a release changes the models.

## RS-499 - The loading freezes, mostly gone

- **Entering a dungeon no longer freezes the page**. The graphics driver builds a real pipeline for every shader the first time it draws it, and the first frame of a dungeon floor asked for all of them at once: on the audit save that one call froze the game for 8.7 seconds. The floor's first compile is now sliced - a few pieces at a time, drawn once off screen, with a frame to the browser between slices - while the loading card holds. Measured on the same save: the longest freeze on dungeon entry fell from **8.7s to 0.84s**, stepping out into the overworld from **4.0s to 1.6s**, and loading into a town from **9.8s to 4.8s** (what remains of the town's is building the world itself, not shaders - that's next).
- **No cost once you're in**: frame rates after loading are unchanged (town 115 fps, dungeon 180 fps on the audit machine, same as before within noise).
- **Shader error checking off in release**: the renderer also no longer reads every shader's compile log, another forced stall.

## RS-498 - Towns run faster: the cloth maths tightened

- **The same folds, a third of the cost**: robes, skirts and cloaks fold exactly as before, but the maths behind them was rewritten to work straight on the mesh data. Recomputing the cloth's lighting normals was a quarter of every frame in a busy town; the skirt refold also recalculated four sines per vertex and the cloak re-measured its own bounds every frame.
- **Measured on a real save** (a city full of tiefling folk, 8-second CPU profiles): 79 to 113 fps, the average frame 12.6 to 8.8 ms, the slowest 5% of frames 17.3 to 11.9 ms. The cloth's share of each frame fell from about 35% to about 17%. On a phone or a weaker PC the same work is saved.
- **Nothing visible changed**: the new normals match the old ones to within rounding (checked edge for edge in the new test), and all the cloth tests pass unchanged.

## RS-497 - Zoom in on yourself

- **Zoom**: the figure in the dye works and at the barber's chair now zooms. Use the mouse wheel, pinch with two fingers on a phone, or tap the **+** and **-** buttons in its corner.
- **Close up on the face**: zooming moves in toward your head, so you can check a hat, a helm's bands or a coif's stitching up close. Drag up or down while zoomed to move along you, down to your boots or back up to your hat. Dragging sideways still turns you round.
- **Right out, as before**: zoomed fully out, the whole figure is framed just as it was.

## RS-496 - A smaller dye works figure

- **A quarter smaller**: your figure in the dye works is now 540 by 659 instead of 720 by 879, leaving more room for the dye rows beside it. It still turns when you drag it and updates as you dye.

## RS-495 - Trim all round, and bigger cape emblems

- **The whole cape perimeter**: a trim dye on a cape now runs all the way round it (sides, top and hem), not just along the bottom. A finer cape's gold edging and its embroidered band of lozenges take the dye too.
- **Patterns take the trim dye**: a robe's stitched runes round the hem now change with its trim band, along with the device on a plate tabard and a wizard hat's glow.
- **Front trim fixed**: the trim strip down the front of a robe or a plate or dragonhide body now follows the shape of the chest. It used to be a flat bar that a fuller chest swallowed, leaving two loose-looking stubs, one under the collar and one at the waist.
- **Bigger cape emblems**: the emblem on the back of a cape is now about two-thirds of the cape's width, up from about half. The vampire skull fills the back without spilling over the edges.

## RS-494 - See yourself at the dye works

- **You, life-size, at the dye works**: the dye works now shows your character on the left of the menu, three times the size of the barber's chair figure. You see what you're wearing as you dye it.
- **Updates as you dye**: every dye, trim, emblem or wash-out shows on the figure at once. Drag it to turn it round and see your cape and its emblem.
- **Fits any screen**: on a smaller window the figure shrinks to fit, and on a phone it sits above the dye rows.

## RS-493 - Dyeable trims

- **Trims take their own dye**: at the dye works, every piece with a trim now has a second row of dyes under it, for the trim alone. That covers a body's trim bands, a great helm's bands, a coif's stitching, a wizard hat's band, a cape's hem and a metal shield's rim.
- **Colour and trim apart**: dye your plate blue with red trim, or a black cape with a gold hem. Washing a trim out is free, and it shows at once.
- **Only where there's a trim**: plain pieces with no trim yet, such as a bronze body, a plain red cape, a quiver or chaps, don't get a trim row. Finer tiers add the trims, as before.

## RS-492 - Your own cape emblem

- **Cape emblem picker**: the dye works now has a **Cape emblem** section beside the shield one. Choose any device you've learned for the back of your cape, or Plain to remove it. Left unchosen, the cape follows your shield's emblem as before.
- **On any cape**: a chosen emblem shows even on a plain Red, Blue or Green cape, embroidered in gold thread unless you dye it. The fire cape keeps its flames.
- **Its own colour**: dye the cape's emblem apart from the shield's. Washing it out is free.
- **Shows at once**: changing an emblem or its colour now redraws your character straight away. Before, it waited until you next changed some gear, so it looked as if nothing had happened.

## RS-491 - Quivers, and the bow on your back

- **Archers start with a quiver**: the Archer kit now comes with a Leather Quiver in the cape slot (+4 ranged accuracy, saves 15% of your arrows).
- **Quivers you can see**: a worn quiver now hangs on your back, slung from the left hip up to the right shoulder, with its mouth by your drawing hand. It is made of turned leather with rolled rims, two buckled straps and a sheaf of fletched arrows. Dizana's Quiver has gold rims.
- **Monster archers carry one too**: goblin and orc archers, and others like them, now wear quivers.
- **The bow slings properly**: a bow put away now lies flat across the middle of your back. Its hidden nocked arrow, longer than the bow itself, had been throwing it off to one side so it floated beside your hip.

## RS-490 - Crossbows shot like crossbows

- **A real crossbow**: crossbows no longer show up as a plain bow. They have their own model: a carved wooden stock swelling to a butt, a prod in the crossbow's metal lashed across the nose, a stirrup, a bone nut, a long trigger lever, and a bolt with a square head and leather vanes in the groove.
- **Shouldered and aimed**: to shoot, you bring it up with the butt in your right shoulder, the right hand on the grip with the elbow out, the left hand under the fore-end, and the body turned side-on. The bolt points straight at the monster, up or down to wherever it stands.
- **Loose and reload**: the string snaps forward, the bolt is gone and the nose kicks up. Then the crossbow comes down, is spanned again, and a fresh bolt is laid in.
- **On time**: the bolt flies, and its hit lands, at the moment of the release, like arrows since RS-489.
- **Carried and stowed**: you carry it by the stock with the nose down, and it goes on your back when you put it away.

## RS-489 - An archer's draw

- **A slower, steadier shot**: drawing a bow now takes about twice as long. The archer draws, holds a moment, then looses, so the whole shot runs just over a second instead of a quick snap.
- **The arrow leaves at the loose**: the arrow flies, and its hit lands, when the string is released, not before it's even drawn. The same goes for monster archers. Spells and crossbows are unchanged.
- **Elbow back and level**: the drawing elbow now sits back along the arrow's line, level with the shoulder, the way a real archer draws, instead of hanging down by the ribs.
- **Side-on stance**: archers turn their body further side-on to the target, with the head turned back to look along the arrow.

## RS-488 - Left-handed bows, aimed at the foe

- **The bow is in the left hand** now, drawn back to the right cheek with the right, the way most archers shoot (a bow with a shield on the left arm still goes in the right).
- **The arrow rests on the thumb**: it lies on top of the bow hand beside the grip, instead of passing through the middle of the fist.
- **Aimed at the target**: your bow arm points at the nearest monster in front of you, up at one on a ledge or down into a pit, and lifts a little more for a long shot. Archers aim the same way at you. With nothing in front, the bow points straight ahead.

## RS-487 - Shooting a bow properly

- **A real archer's shot, for you and every archer you fight:**
  - **Stance:** they turn side-on to the target and look down the arrow, the bow arm straight out at the mark.
  - **The draw:** the drawing hand takes the string at the bow and draws it all the way back to the cheek.
  - **The release:** the string snaps back straight, the arrow is gone, and the hand flies on past the cheek, a beat before the bow is lowered.
- **The string bends:** drawn, the string runs from each tip of the bow back to the fingers, instead of staying a straight stick.
- **An arrow on the string:** while the bow is drawn an arrow is nocked on it, from the fingers through the grip, its head just past the bow, until it's loosed and flies.

## RS-486 - Still shadows

- **No more fluttering:** out on the land and in towns, the sun turned smoothly across the sky with the clock. Every time the shadows were redrawn, every few frames, they landed a hair differently, and their edges fluttered. The sun now moves in tiny half-degree steps, about one every two and a half seconds, so between steps the shadows hold perfectly still. Over the day they still swing round as before.

## RS-485 - Towns that look like their people

- **Every race builds its own way, on the land:**
  - **Humans:** plaster houses under red tiles, round a stone tower.
  - **Half-elves:** blue slate roofs among trees, with a windmill.
  - **High elves:** white stone towers under slender blue spires.
  - **Wood elves:** huts round a great tree, with a platform in its boughs.
  - **Dwarves:** stone houses with smoking chimneys, before a hall door cut into a great rock.
  - **Orcs:** hide tents behind spiked stakes, round a skull-topped totem.
  - **Gnomes:** round houses under spotted mushroom caps, by a clockwork tower.
  - **Dragonborn:** terraced sandstone halls round a great brazier.
  - **Tieflings:** dark stone houses with violet windows under an obsidian spire and its lantern.
  - **Halflings:** keep their hobbit holes.
- **Size you can see:** a village is a few houses behind a low fence. A town has more, behind a palisade. A city has more again, behind a stone wall with gate towers, round a grander landmark, and stands bigger on the land.
- **The name plate says what it is:** under a town's name, for example "dwarven · city".

## RS-484 - Paste a whole message

- **The paste box finds the code itself:** you can paste a whole copied message, words, line breaks and all. The save code is picked out of it, so in the Claude app you can copy a chat message holding a code and paste it straight in.

## RS-483 - Import that works on phones

- **The import button is a real file picker:** on a phone, tapping **IMPORT a save file** could do nothing. The picker was opened by a hidden button, which some apps ignore, and it only allowed files marked as JSON, so a downloaded save the phone hadn't labelled that way was greyed out. Now the button is a picker of its own and takes any file. A file that isn't a save is refused with a message.
- **Save codes in a file work too:** a text file holding a save code imports the same way.

## RS-482 - Save codes: copy and paste your adventurer

- **Move a character with copy and paste:** some apps (the Claude app on a phone) can't open a file picker or save a file, so exporting and importing didn't work there. Every save now has a **copy save code** button, on the title screen and in the pause menu. It turns the whole adventurer into one block of text and copies it.
- **Paste it anywhere you play:** on the title screen, **PASTE a save code** brings the adventurer back in that app or browser. Pasting a save file's text works too.
- **If the copy doesn't take:** the code is also shown in a box, so you can press and hold to select it and copy it by hand.

## RS-481 - Faster dungeons

- **Your lantern casts no shadows:** since RS-480 it no longer cast yours, and redrawing its shadows in all six directions on every step as you walked was the heaviest part of a dungeon frame. The torches throw the shadows now.
- **Still rooms cost nothing:** the torches' shadows are redrawn only while something moves (you, a monster, an ally) and for a moment after. They used to be redrawn every other frame whatever happened.
- **The numbers:** in a player's save, two floors down a dungeon, a frame went from 5.7 ms and 577 draw calls to 4.5 ms and 246 standing still, and from 5.7 ms and 718 calls to 4.9 ms and 444 walking.

## RS-480 - Natural shadows in the dungeon

- **No more black blot beside you:** the hard, dark shadow next to your character was your own lantern's, held up over your shoulder. With the dimmer dungeon light of RS-479 it had gone nearly black. Your lantern no longer throws your shadow. It still lights you and casts the shadows of walls and props further off.
- **Torchlit shadows:** your shadows now come from the torches around you, falling away from each one and filled in by your lantern's light, the way they would by real torchlight.
- **Softer edges:** torch and lantern shadows are blurred at the edge, as a flame's are.

## RS-479 - Moodier, punchier dungeons

- **Lit by the torches:** below ground the fill light is dimmer and a faint cold blue, and the torches are stronger. Pools of warm, flickering light now carry each room, with real darkness between them.
- **Every kind of place its own light:**
  - **Plain dungeons:** cool grey stone.
  - **Mines:** warm amber.
  - **Graveyards and necromancer towers:** sickly green-grey.
  - **Sunken temples:** cold violet.
  - **Castles:** warm torchlit stone.
  - **The pyramid:** dusty gold.
  - **Dragon lairs:** red-lit.
- **Blows you can feel, without shaking the screen:**
  - **Hits:** a blow throws sparks off armour, golems and stone, bone chips off skeletons, slime off oozes, a cold wisp off spirits, and blood from flesh.
  - **Misses:** a puff of dust where the blow fell short.
  - **Kills:** a bigger burst.
  - **Crits and kills:** a beat's pause, where everything all but freezes for a moment.
- **The camera leans in:** when foes are close and in sight, the camera eases in a little, and drifts back out when the fight is over.

## RS-478 - Leather coifs finer each tier

- **The coif grows grander, like the metal helms and wizard's hats:**
  - **Leather:** plain.
  - **Hard Leather:** a stitched seam over the crown and down the back.
  - **Studded:** rows of rivet studs on both sides.
  - **Green d'hide:** a feather tuft sweeping back from the side.
  - **Blue d'hide:** overlapping dragon scales over the crown.
  - **Red d'hide:** silver thread, and a silver clasp with a red gem at the throat.
  - **Black d'hide:** black dragon spines along the ridge.
  Each piece sits on the hood's own surface, in the hide's own colour.

## RS-477 - Back swipe closes menus

- **The back gesture doesn't close the game any more:** on Android, the side swipe (or the Back button) now works like Escape. It closes whatever menu is open, and in play it opens the pause menu, instead of leaving the page and losing your place. A browser's Back button on a computer does the same.
- **Still a way out:** on the title screen the swipe is let through, so a second swipe there leaves as usual.

## RS-476 - Staves finer each grade

- **Magic staves grow grander, in their element's colour:**
  - **Plain staff:** as it was.
  - **Battlestaff:** a glowing orb round its crystal, held in a silver cup of four prongs, with a band on the shaft.
  - **Mystic staff:** three tall gold claws cradling a bigger orb, gold bands, and a gold spiral wound up the shaft.
  - **Master and Ancient staves:** a dark horned crown over the orb and three rune stones circling it. The Ancient Staff glows violet.
- **Melee quarterstaffs stay plain wood.**

## RS-475 - Cloth armour finer each tier

- **Wizard hats grow grander:**
  - **Wizard:** plain.
  - **Splitbark:** a band.
  - **Mystic:** a glowing blue gem.
  - **Infinity:** a gold band, an amber gem and stars.
  - **Ahrim's:** a green gem, a crescent moon, and a tall tip crooked back.
  The Visage of the Sage keeps its own look.
- **The hem runes shine:** on Infinity and Ahrim's robes the runes round the hem glow, Ahrim's the brightest.
- **Tomes carry your emblem:** a tome's covers are inlaid with the emblem you chose at the dye works, in its dye. The finest tomes bear a star if you haven't chosen one; plain leaves them bare.

## RS-474 - A truer vampire skull

- **Redrawn as a real skull:** the vampire skull emblem has proper anatomy now:
  - **Cranium:** a high dome narrowing at sunken temples, with a crack across the crown.
  - **Eyes:** a heavy brow ridge over deep, round, separate eye sockets, each with a red gleam inside.
  - **Face:** hollow cheeks under the cheekbones, and a nose opening shaped like an upside-down heart.
  - **Teeth and jaw:** an upper row of teeth with two long fangs, and a dropped lower jaw with its own teeth. The bone is shaded cream to ochre.
- **Sharper everywhere:** the collar is shaded from its fold, the silver ring and hexagram are engraved, and the whole emblem is drawn at twice the detail on shields.

## RS-473 - The vampire skull

- **A reward emblem:** once you can wear dragon armour (Defence 60), the dye works offers the Vampire Skull. It's a fanged skull with its jaw dropped, set on a high crimson collar, inside a silver occult ring crossed by a hexagram.
- **On shields and capes:** it's painted across the face of your kite or tower shield, following its curve, and large on the back of your cape. On a red shield the collar turns black so it still stands out.
- **Dye it:** the emblem dye colours its collar.

## RS-472 - Capes finer each tier

- **Capes grow grander with their level, like the shields:** a plain cape is plain cloth. The next tier adds a trimmed hem, then your shield emblem on its back, then gold edging, a fur collar, an embroidered band of lozenges, and at the top its gold thread glows. It's all painted on the cloth, so it ripples and swings with the cape.
- **Your emblem on your back:** the emblem you chose for your shields (and the dye you gave it) is on your cape too. Plain leaves the cape bare. Unchosen, the cape bears a cross, a chevron, a star or a sun for its tier.
- **The Fire Cape is fire:** molten rock with glowing lava pools and cracks, and a dark fur collar.
- **Which tier:** plain capes are the lowest, then black capes, Ava's, Obsidian, the god and skill capes, Legends and the Quest Point Cape. The Fire Cape and the Cape of Accomplishment are the finest. Quivers stay quivers.

## RS-471 - Dye your shield's emblem

- **The emblem takes a dye:** the dye works' SHIELD EMBLEM section has a colour row. Use any dye you carry to paint the emblem on all your metal shields, kite and tower, whichever emblem it is. The dye is used up; washing it out is free and brings back the tier's own colour.
- **With the shield's own dye:** dye the shield one colour and its emblem another.

## RS-470 - Choose your shield's emblem

- **Your own device:** the dye works has a new SHIELD EMBLEM row. Pick the emblem painted on all your metal shields, kite and tower alike. It keeps the colour your shield's tier gives it.
- **Learned with the metals:** each metal you can wear (Defence) or forge (Smithing) teaches you another emblem:
  - **From the start:** plain, a cross and a bend (bronze and iron both need only Defence 1).
  - **Steel:** a chevron.
  - **Mithril:** a saltire.
  - **Adamant:** a sun.
  - **Rune:** an eight-pointed star.
  - **Dragon:** a double chevron.
- **Or leave it to the smith:** choose "the smith's own" and each shield bears its tier's usual device again.

## RS-469 - Tower shields

- **A new shield in every metal:** the Tower Shield is a tall, squared shield that curves round your body like a Roman scutum. It's strapped to your forearm like the kiteshield, with the same cross, chevron, saltire, boss and spikes as you go up the tiers.
- **Heavy defence, at a price:** it gives half as much defence again as the kiteshield of its metal (a Dragon one gives 96 against 64). In exchange you aim worse with a blade or a bow, by 5 at bronze rising to 17 at dragon, and it costs twice the kiteshield's magic accuracy.
- **Where to get one:** forge it at the anvil from four bars of the metal, or find one; about 3 in 10 metal shields that drop are tower shields.
- **Balance:** the gear and economy sims give the same results as before; they kit fighters with kiteshields, so tower shields are an extra choice.

## RS-468 - Wood elves look ahead

- **Head level again:** a wood elf's head was tipped about 23 degrees up toward the sky. The head was lifted to make up for the curve of its stooped back, and lifted far too much. Now its stooped back stays, and its face looks straight ahead, standing or walking, the way anyone else's does.

## RS-467 - Shields worn on the forearm

- **Strapped on like a real shield:** a shield sits on the outside of your left forearm, the arm along its back, your fist near its top edge and its point past your elbow. It used to be held out on the end of your fist like a tray.
- **Raised in a fight:** your forearm stands upright in front of your chest, so the shield covers your body, its face to the foe and its point down.
- **Carried at your side:** walking with it out, it rides low at your left side, point down, turned a little outward.
- **Everyone:** knights, goblins and every other shield-bearer hold theirs the same way. It still goes on your back when you put your weapon away.

## RS-466 - Knightly helms and shields

- **Closed helms:** every metal full helm is now a closed great helm like the knights wear, and it gets finer with each tier:
  - **Bronze:** plain.
  - **Iron:** polished bands round its rim and crown, and a strip down its face.
  - **Steel:** a red plume.
  - **Mithril:** a white plume.
  - **Adamant:** gold bands.
  - **Rune:** gold wings.
  - **Dragon:** curved horns in gold sockets and a dark red crest.
  The old kettle, dwarf, winged and horned helms are gone from players' metal helms.
- **Real kite shields:** every metal kiteshield is now a metal-faced kite shield, finer each tier:
  - **Bronze:** plain.
  - **Iron:** a cross in a darker tone.
  - **Steel:** a silver cross.
  - **Mithril:** a gold cross and a gold rim.
  - **Adamant:** a chevron and a domed boss.
  - **Rune:** a saltire.
  - **Dragon:** a chevron, a boss and spikes round its rim.
  Bronze used to show a round shield.
- **Dyes still work:** a dyed helm or shield keeps its new look in the dye's colour.

## RS-465 - Two-handed weapons swung with both hands

- **Both hands on the weapon:** greatswords, great axes, halberds, spears, tridents and quarterstaffs are now held and swung with both hands. The left hand closes on the grip below the right hand, or further down the shaft, and stays there through the guard and the whole blow. It used to dangle beside the body.
- **A blow for each weapon:**
  - **Greatsword:** heaved up over the right shoulder, then cleaved down and across.
  - **Great axe and halberd:** raised straight overhead and brought down in a chop.
  - **Spear and trident:** drawn back, then driven forward in a low thrust, the point levelling as it goes.
  - **Quarterstaff:** swung round in a wide sweep.
- **Still one-handed:** a wizard's staff, and a spear carried with a shield.
- **Plain wood:** a quarterstaff no longer sparkles with a wizard's motes.

## RS-464 - Bolts, darts and knives can be made

- **The missing recipes:** bolts, darts, throwing knives, broad bolts and fire arrows had recipes, but no bench showed them, so they couldn't be made anywhere. They're now at the fletching bench in the Tailor's workshop, beside the arrows. Bolts take one metal bar for 12; darts take one bar for 15 and knives one bar for 8.
- **Every recipe has a bench:** a new check makes sure no recipe is ever stranded like this again.

## RS-463 - Tooltips that tell the truth

- **Runes:** no tooltip sends you to the alchemist any more; he stopped selling runes in RS-369. A rune's tooltip now lists the altar that binds it and where that altar stands, the temple's rune counter and its price (for the basic five), and the combat level monsters start dropping it.
- **Real combat levels:** dragonhide colours, big bones and rune drops were quoted on the old 1-20 tier scale. They now give real combat levels.
- **No more "undefined":** the Ancient oak's line, and the "heals undefined" on antipoisons and weapon poisons, now read properly. Scrolls no longer claim to hold a spell.
- **Crafting vs Fletching:** bows, arrows and bolts now show the Fletching level they need, not a Crafting level. Arrows are made at the fletching bench, and gold bars are cast at the jeweller's bench, not forged at an anvil.
- **Spells:** R switches your ranged mode to magic; you cast by clicking a foe. Every "R casts it" hint now says so. Lunar spells say they need the Lunar book (Magic 65, Defence 40).
- **Skill guides:**
  - Slayer xp is three times the monster's life.
  - Minion counts include the level-50 perk: 2 at 30, 3 at 50, 4 at 60.
  - The zombie lasts 10 turns longer (it isn't slow or tough), and the ghost never misses.
  - Tuna is caught only out at sea.
  - Runite never turns up in hill rocks.
  - Aggressive gives +1 damage with a bow, not +2.
- **Help and town text:**
  - The waypoint lists every town you've visited; there's nothing to touch.
  - The stash is now the bank (240 items).
  - The bounty board hangs on the tavern's wall.
  - Tools come from the Peddler.
  - Arrows are fletched or looted.
  - The journal is on ; only.
  - Longrange is an attack style.
  - Camping restores prayer, and its danger is a monster that comes for you.
  - Each race's trait is listed correctly.
  - The orb kiln's description, and the shore, platform and Mining Guild lines, are fixed.

## RS-462 - The D&D ability scores are gone

- **No more STR to CHA:** the six ability scores, left over from the old rules, are off the character sheet and out of your save. They had been stuck at 10 for a long time; your skills are what count.
- **Old gear bonuses kept in a form that works:** a score bonus on a piece you own becomes the gear bonus it was worth. Strength becomes strength, Dexterity becomes ranged and attack, Intelligence and Wisdom become magic, and Constitution becomes defence. "All attributes" becomes all five. Charisma never did anything and is simply dropped. Wisdom used to do nothing, so pieces with it now help your magic.
- **No new score bonuses:** an awakening weapon now always rolls a bonus that does something.
- **Old sets too:** the Vindicator's, Nightshade and Stormcaller's set bonuses give the same gear bonuses instead of scores.

## RS-461 - Cloaked towns run smoothly again

- **Cloth where you can see it:** since RS-458, every cloak in a town ran the full cloth simulation. A tiefling town, where nearly everyone wears a cloak, spent about 33 ms of every frame on it and dropped to around 15 to 40 fps. Now you and anyone within a few steps of you wear real cloth. Folk further off swing their cloaks the old way, which looks the same from across a square.
- **Lighter robes:** folk away from you refold their robes and cloaks every other frame.
- **Faster cloth:** the cloth solver itself does less work for the same result.
- **The numbers:** in a tiefling town from a player's save, 14 fps became 49 fps (at a sharper resolution), and a dragonborn town went from 37 to 51 fps.

## RS-460 - The Sage's hat takes a dye

- **Dyeing the Visage of the Sage works:** the hat's indigo was fixed in place, so a dye was used up but never showed. Now it takes the dye's colour the moment you dye it, and keeps its band, gem, moon and stars. Washing it out brings the indigo back.
- **The dye works shows it right:** its colour swatch shows the hat's real colour.

## RS-459 - Calmer cloaks, draped over tails

- **No more wild bouncing:** turning on the spot, lunging or running fast used to whip the cloak round faster than it could follow, so it bounced about and went through you. Now it follows a fast body at a cloth's pace, and comes to rest when it touches you instead of bouncing off.
- **It stays on your back:** part of the back had no surface for the cloth to rest on, so it could fall straight through. It now rests on the whole back, with room for a stooped back.
- **Draped over tails:** a dragonborn's or a tiefling's cloak now lies over the tail, not through it, standing or walking.

## RS-458 - Cloaks are cloth

- **Real cloth physics:** a cloak is now a sheet of cloth, simulated every frame. It is pinned at the neck and shoulders, and the rest hangs under its own weight with air drag.
- **It moves with you:** walk and it trails out and lifts behind you. Turn and it swings round. Stop and it settles back into its folds, with a little breeze stirring the hem.
- **It stays outside you:** the cloth rests against your back, and your hips and striding legs push it out behind them, so it never passes through your body.
- **Sitting still drapes it:** seated, the cloak lies over the seat as before.

## RS-457 - Cloaks pinned at the shoulders

- **No more floating cloaks.** As you walked, your cloak swung out as one stiff sheet from its top edge. That lifted it off your shoulders and left it floating behind them. Now it's pinned at the neck and shoulders: its top stays laid over them, and only the part below your shoulder blades billows out, more the further down, so it bends like cloth. Sitting at your campfire it drapes the same way.

## RS-456 - The sage's hat

- **Uniques made of the right stuff.** A unique piece of armour took a random base, so the Visage of the Sage, all magic, could come out as a steel Full Helm. Now what a unique is made of follows its name and its strongest stat:
  - a magic name like the Sage's, or mostly magic stats, makes it cloth: a robe, a wizard's hat;
  - a ranger's name, or mostly ranged stats, makes it hide;
  - anything else makes it metal.
  Its stats are unchanged. What it looks like and the skill it asks for follow its material. Unique pieces you already own are put right as your save loads.
- **The Visage of the Sage's own look.** It's a deep indigo wizard's hat with a gold band round the brim, a pale blue gem glowing at its front, a gold crescent moon on the cone and little gold stars scattered over it.

## RS-455 - One way to make an orb

- **No orb from glass.** The kiln had two recipes for the same unpowered orb: one from a silver bar and one from molten glass. The glass one is gone. You make an unpowered orb from a silver bar and charge it with runes, as before.

## RS-454 - Feats removed

- **No more feats.** The old D&D ability score improvements and feats haven't been offered since your level started coming from your skills, but their code and their effects were still in the game. They're gone now: the screens, the Lucky reroll, Tough's extra life, Bonded Spirit and the rest. A character saved with feats from long ago loads and plays as normal, just without them.

## RS-453 - Fair upgrade arrows, bonuses on show

- **Arrows judge the gear itself.** The green and red arrows counted a piece's random bonuses heavily. A single +17% damage bonus outweighed a whole tier of metal, so a better weapon you'd just forged showed as a downgrade from an old one that had grown bonuses. Now the arrows compare only a piece's own stats: damage, attack, strength, defence, magic, ranged and prayer.
- **Bonuses in their own box.** A piece's bonuses, whether rolled when it dropped or grown on a weapon as it kills, sit in a violet box of their own under its stats, so you can see them at a glance and judge them yourself.
- **How far a weapon has awakened.** A weapon you fight with grows a new bonus at 50, 150 and 300 kills. Its tooltip now shows its kill count, how many bonuses it has grown, and when the next one comes.

## RS-452 - Solid stairs

- **Monsters keep out of the stairs.** A monster chasing you would walk straight into a flight of stairs, or the way out, and stand there in it. Stairs and ways out are now solid to monsters: they come round them to you, and any that start in one step off it.
- **Nothing lies in the stairs.** An ore vein, cave pool, herb patch or chest can no longer end up inside a flight of stairs, on a new floor or on one you've been to before.

## RS-451 - A giant crab

- **Giant crabs are giant.** The giant crab was sized like a giant rat or bat, a little smaller than a person. Now it's a great armoured beast nearly twice that size, more than a tile across. King crabs are the same size. Ordinary crabs stay small.

## RS-450 - Skeleton archers' arrows

- **See the arrows fly.** When your skeleton archer shot, you only saw the hit land on its target. Now each arrow flies from the archer to its foe, as yours do, and sticks in what it hits.

## RS-449 - A line in the cave pool, the tome put away

- **Fishing line in the caves.** Fishing a cave pool down in a dungeon, you held the rod but no line came off it. Now the line runs from the rod's tip down to the pool, sagging, with a red float bobbing on the water, as it does on the open land.
- **The tome shuts with your weapon.** A spell tome only shut out of a fight, so it could stand open in your hand while your staff was on your back. Now it shuts whenever your weapon is put away, and opens again when you draw it.

## RS-448 - The whole counter serves you

- **Click anywhere on the counter.** Inside a shop, only the shopkeeper themselves could be clicked or tapped, so clicking the counter beside them just walked you to the floor nearby. Now every part of the counter opens the shop: you step up to it and are served.

## RS-447 - The rose's points close up

- **No gaps round the dais.** Each point of the compass rose was cut a little narrower than its share of the circle, so slivers of cobbles showed between neighbouring points just outside the waypoint's bottom step. The points now meet edge to edge all the way round.

## RS-446 - The rose without its band

- **The right piece taken out.** RS-445 removed the waypoint's wide bottom step, but the part poking through was the round stone band at the foot of the compass rose, showing between its points. The step is back, so there are three round steps again, and the band is gone. The rose's points now run in under the bottom step.

## RS-445 - The rose as the bottom step

- **No step poking through.** The waypoint's wide bottom step stuck out through the stone compass rose. It's gone: the rose is the bottom step now, with the two round steps, the runes and the crystal standing on it.

## RS-444 - The compass rose in stone

- **A rose you can stand on.** The compass rose round the waypoint was paint on the paving. Now it's cut in stone: eight bevelled points, the four quarters longer, each split down its spine into dark slate and pale stone, round a band where they meet. It's a step of its own, so the waypoint's three round steps, its runes and its floating crystal all stand one step higher on top of it.

## RS-443 - Real cobblestones

- **Fine cobbles in town.** The town's paving was painted into the ground at only a few pixels a tile, so the cobbles were blurry blots in the same six-by-six grid on every tile. Now the paved streets and the square are laid with a fine cobble texture of their own:
  - stones laid in courses of different heights, each stone its own width and shape;
  - shaded as they bulge, worn smooth on top, a crack here and there;
  - set in dark mortar with moss in the joints.
  The pattern runs on across the tiles and only repeats every four, so there's no grid to see. The compass rose round the waypoint is painted sharp on top of it.

## RS-442 - Trinkets look like charms

- **No more mistaking a trinket for a necklace.** A trinket's icon was a gem pendant on a cord, much like an amulet's, though trinkets have a slot of their own. Now it's a charm: a curved claw bound in leather to a small iron ring, with beads and a feather tied on. The binding is in the trinket's own colour.

## RS-441 - Windows that fit

- **Braces clear of the windows.** On the half-timbered houses, the diagonal braces were set in a checkerboard that ignored where the windows were. On a narrow house like the Alchemist they ran straight across the glass. Now a brace only goes in a bay without a window.
- **Shutters that open properly.** Both shutters on a window used to angle the same way, and on a side wall they stuck straight out from it. Now they swing open either way, close against the wall. This fixes every timber house in town, not just the Alchemist.

## RS-440 - The XP tracker while you cook

- **Cooking shows on the XP tracker.** The tracker only showed out in the world and hid whenever a menu was open. The cooking panel stays open while you cook, so you never saw your cooking xp climb. Now the tracker shows while you're working through a batch, whether cooking at a fire or a hearth, or smelting, forging, brewing or crafting at a bench. While you cook it sits below the small cooking card in the corner, and it goes back to its place when you're done.

## RS-439 - Slower crafting, a better seat by the fire

- **Every craft takes twice as long.** Cooking, smelting, forging, brewing, crafting and the rest now take 1.3 seconds a piece instead of 0.65. Each piece of food has its time on the grill or in the pan, and long batches give you time to watch them.
- **Back from the fire.** At your own campfire you sit a little further from the flames, on your stool across the fire from the camera.
- **The marshmallow the right way up.** Your toasting stick pointed down into the fire. Now you hold it out level or a little up, with the marshmallow over the flames.

## RS-438 - The boar's death

- **A boar dies like a boar.** A dead giant boar's head was wrung round on its neck and its mouth hung open in a long red trough, and its legs folded up against its belly. Now it goes down onto its side with its legs lying out, its mouth shut and its head in line with its neck. Every four-legged beast's head turns less as it falls, and their knees stay clear of their bodies too.
- **The boar's jaw fits.** Even alive, a boar's lower jaw was a slab hanging below its snout, with the red of its mouth showing between them from below or the side. It's raised to meet the snout now, and made as wide as the mouth it closes.

## RS-437 - Meetings you can see

- **People on the road are there to be seen.** The lost child, the travelling wizard, the sandwich lady, the genie, the dwarf and the highwaymen used to spring on you out of nowhere as you walked. Now, when one turns up, they're a few leagues off, by the road if there's one near, and the log tells you which way. They wait there, as figures you can see with a ? over them, for you to walk up and meet them. If you don't, they move on after a while.
- **The skeleton with the note.** The skeleton in rusted mail that starts *A Note on the Bones* now lies where you can see it, half-buried by the path with the note in its hand and its sword beside it. Walk onto it to search it.
- **The weeping ghost.** On a night when you pass a graveyard, the ghost that starts *The Weeping Ghost* hovers at the graveyard's edge where you can see it.

## RS-436 - Quest places you can see

- **Story quests mark real places.** When a story quest sent you somewhere out in the wilds, all you found there was a floating ! over empty grass. Now there's something to find:
  - the Goblin King's hall, a long timber hall with a turf roof, tusks over the door and skulls on pikes;
  - the goblins' war camp of hide tents round a fire, under a green banner;
  - the hermit's thatched stone hut with its woodpile;
  - a lonely grave with bones in the grass and a pale ghost over it;
  - broken ruins with a fallen arch and toppled columns;
  - a dig site with its pit, spoil heap and spade;
  - a ring of standing stones round a crystal crackling with the storm, for the mages and the Stormcaller;
  - the thieves' cave in a crag, with crates outside and a lantern;
  - the Herald's black obelisk with green fire at its foot;
  - and a cairn with a signpost for any other step.
  The ! still floats above, so you can spot it from afar.

## RS-435 - Gilded armour can be salvaged

- **The loot filter takes Gilded pieces.** Gilded armour from treasure trails counted as a rare find, and the loot filter never salvages anything rare or better, so a Gilded Full Helm stayed in your bag however you set the filter. It's only ordinary tier armour with gold paint and a little extra defence, so the filter now judges it by its tier and bonuses like any other piece. Genuinely rare drops, set pieces, Uniques, relics and locked items are still never salvaged.

## RS-434 - Nothing springs from nowhere

- **Ambushes you can see coming.** Two kinds of fight used to start out of nowhere. One was the ambush while you camped in the open, a fight you couldn't flee. The other was the 1-in-80 chance that something crept up while you gathered. Now each is a real wandering monster. It shows up a few leagues off, and the log says what it is and which way it's coming from. Then it comes for you. You can stand and fight when it arrives, or get away.
- **Hunters close in.** A monster hunting you heads straight for you and finds its way round trees, rocks and water. It takes a step for every step of yours and for every stroke of your work, and it stops your work when it's right on you. Outrun it by fourteen leagues or so and it loses your trail and goes back to roaming.
- **Camping when one finds you.** You get no rest that night, but you're no longer pinned in a fight you can't leave.

## RS-433 - X marks the spot

- **Treasure maps and clue scrolls are marked.** Reading a treasure map, or a clue scroll that sends you somewhere, said the spot was on your map, but all it did was lift the fog around it, with nothing to show where the spot actually was. Now there's an X: a red one in a parchment ring on the world map, one on the minimap, and on the land itself red paint on the ground with a red X turning slowly above it, so you can see it from a distance. Stand on it and read the map to dig up the casket, or read the clue to move the trail on.

## RS-432 - Frogs, birds and beasts fall properly

- **Frogs and birds slump.** A dead frog or toad tipped over onto its side and lay there, and a bird like the giant vulture stood on its legs through the floor. Now they slump where they die, belly down: a frog's legs splay out flat, and a bird folds its legs under it, pitches onto its breast and lets its wings droop to the ground.
- **Beasts keep their legs out.** A giant boar's short legs folded up inside its belly as it fell, and a wolf's tucked in. Now a beast's legs stay outside its body and fold less, and it sinks onto its legs as it goes down before it rolls onto its side.
- **Steadier hips.** The hip limit from RS-427 could slip for a moment as a body hit the floor. It now holds through the whole fall.

## RS-431 - Finer armour, tier by tier

- **Every tier looks finer than the last.** Your body armour now builds up as you climb the tiers: each keeps everything the tier below had and adds something more.
  - **Plate:** shoulder pads (Bronze), rivets on the breastplate (Iron), polished trim on the pads, collar and belt (Steel), a tabard down the front (Mithril), an emblem on it (Adamant), gold trim and a gold emblem (Rune), and black spikes on a dark tabard (Dragon).
  - **Leather and dragonhide:** plain (Leather), shoulder pads (Hard), studs (Studded), trim in a lighter tone of the hide (Green), a fur collar (Blue), silver trim (Red), and spikes with a dark fur collar (Black).
  - **Robes:** a plain robe and sash (Wizard), a trim band at the hem and collar (Splitbark), glowing blue runes (Mystic), gold trim and gold runes (Infinity), and a shoulder mantle edged in gold with green runes (Ahrim's).
- **Trim that moves with the robe.** A robe's hem band is stitched into the skirt like its runes, so it swings with it. It used to be a stiff ring round the hips that hid inside the skirt's flare.

## RS-430 - Runes stitched into the robe
